10 Pokémon Battles That Break the Rules of the Games

📅 2026-11-03  |  Read Source →

Laurence Wall is a Contributor at DualShockers who has been writing professionally since 2022 and covering games since 2023. He primarily writes guides and lists, with a focus on indie games, RPGs, ARPGs, Soulslikes, MMOs, card games, boss fights, and detailed tutorials. Before joining DualShockers, Laurence worked as

Pokémon games have never really been much of a challenge. I mean, that’s why Nuzlockes are so popular, right? You need to artificially inject some difficulty into the titles to really make the formula work.

However, while most trainers and Pokémon in the series follow the rules, others sometimes break them in one way or another. Intentionally or otherwise, this creates some of the more interesting challenges as the devs try to balance scales that are invariably tipped in our favour.

Let’s look at some of the most interesting examples, and why they make for such fascinating encounters.

This one is pretty famous, especially if you’ve ever Nuzlocked Ultra Sun and Moon! But why does this battle break the rules?

For starters, this battle is more like a trainer battle: you can’t run from this fight or catch Necrozma. No, it must be defeated! And if that wasn’t annoying enough, as soon as you enter the fight, Necrozma gains +1 to every stat.

On top of that, Necrozma has great coverage with its moveset and the ability Neuroforce, making its super-effective attacks even more potent. The thing is, you might have seen inherent stat boosts before, but they were from Totem battles! Necrozma isn’t even a totem Pokémon, and still gets a ridiculous amount of buffs. Getting through this battle without losing a single Pokémon is a massive challenge.

This list covers some of the best Pokémon ROM hacks to attempt a nuzlocke challenge.

I remember the first time I played Pokémon Yellow and ran into the Ghost. Honestly, the whole vibe of Lavender Town and the Pokémon Tower really freaked me out. But then again, I think I was like 4 or 5! Now this Ghost isn’t a Gastly; it isn’t even a Pokémon… not until you have the Silph Scope at least!

Of course, we all know it’s a grieving Marowak, but oddly enough, even though this really should be a regular Pokémon battle, you can’t catch this Marowak, no matter how hard you try. At least Necrozma gets an excuse; Marowak isn’t even legendary.

The thing is, though, in the original Generation 1 games, you can use a Poke Doll to escape the battle. If you do it this way, the battle is considered over, and you're the victor.

The Pokémon Mystery Dungeon series is pretty infamous for having ridiculously unfair battles that pit you and your partner against a massive number of opponents. Whether it's the entire cast of your Guild or Darkrai and 6 other Pokémon from the Dungeon surrounding you! But you always come out on top!

Well, one of the most annoying things that always seems to happen in these games is that your victory often means very little. For example, in the Grovyle fight at Crystal Lake, it doesn’t matter whether you win or lose; the story plays out as if you lost.

The same is true of the battle against Snover in Bidoof’s Special Episode. Even if you find a way to win, the game assumes you lost, so putting in extra effort and using items in these fights is pretty pointless.

Generation 5 is pretty famous for having Pokémon with stupidly high evolution levels. Whether it’s Mienfoo at level 50, Pawniard at level 52 or Larvesta at level 59. Of course, the most famous of these is Zweilous, the Pseudo-Legendary of the games, which evolves into Hydreigon at an eye-watering level 64.

The thing is, if you remember playing the original Black and White and facing off against Ghetsis at the end, you’ll remember he has a Hydreigon as his ace. And, for some magical reason, it's level 54. On the one hand, I get Game Freak wanting to give cool, powerful Pokémon to trainers and give us a challenge, but 10 whole levels under? Whatever happened to treating your Pokémon with love and respect?

Ah, and speaking of having underleveled Pokémon, we have another cheater in our midst. The thing is, Lance actually commits another assortment of sins alongside having illegal Pokémon.

Let’s ignore his 3 underleveled Dragonite for now, and take a look at his Generation 1 and 2 movesets. First, as part of the Elite 4, Lance has a Dragonite with Barrier, a move it cannot learn in that generation. He shoots himself in the foot a little with this, however, as the battle AI is coded to always use super-effective moves, even if they don’t actually do any damage. So he will always use Barrier against, let’s say, a Gengar, because of its poison typing.

And he commits another error in the next Johto titles, where he has an Aerodactyl with Rock Slide, another move that can’t be learned in Generation 2. Either Lance is a huge cheater, or maybe he just knows some secrets that we don't!

This list covers the hardest Elite Four members from Generations 1-9 of the Pokémon games.

While Generation 8 certainly wasn’t my favourite, it did have some odd yet memorable moments. One of these shows up when you fight Opal, the Fairy-type gym leader of Ballonlea.

This battle introduces some fairly bizarre mechanics, not found anywhere else in the series. As the battle goes on, Opal will ask you questions about herself. If you get them right, then great, you’ll get a nice little stat boost. But if you get them wrong, your Pokémon's stats will actually decrease. Yep, not from a move or an item, but a piece of trivia, mid-fight.

And the thing is, the last of these isn’t even true. You have to lie to Opal, saying she looks younger than she actually is, to get the final stat boost! Of course, this fight wasn’t actually difficult, but it’s still an odd event!

And speaking of Generation 8, we have another major cheater over here. You’ll meet Avery in your adventure through the Isle of Armour DLC for Pokémon Shield.

Avery considers himself a bit of a rival, and as such, he will do anything to win. In the 3rd trial, you’ll battle Avery again; however, in this match, he will set up Psychic Terrain before the fight even begins!

Psychic Terrain is a pretty strong move that boosts the power of all Psychic moves by 50%, and it usually takes a full turn to set up. Unless, of course, Avery had a Tapu Lele… which he doesn’t! He realises this is wrong afterwards and pays the price! Good riddance, eh?

If you have Pokémon Sword, instead of facing off against Avery, you’ll meet Klara, who fills a similar role, acting as your rival as you progress through the DLC.

Like the previous encounter, during the 3rd Trial, Klara will scatter a load of Toxic Spikes on the field before the battle even begins, which feels a bit more devious. And this isn’t just 1 layer of spikes, but 2, meaning that you’ll have the Toxic variant instead of the regular poison! Because Toxic Spikes doesn’t wear off like Psychic Terrain, you’ll need to Rapid Spin these away, or send out your own Poison-type to absorb the poison.

The funny thing about these cheaters is that I actually really like this added challenge in Pokémon games. Lately, the titles have become laughably easy, perhaps because we’re a little older. So having your opponents cheat and get extra turns certainly levels the playing field a little bit.

Pokémon Colosseum and XD are some of my favourite Pokémon games of all time. They brought so much personality to the Pokémon animations, and the world feels much more alive and dangerous than in other titles.

At the end of XD, you’ll face off against Greevil, and realise he actually gets 7 Pokémon instead of 6! First off, you’ll have to face the awesome Shadow Lugia, XD001. If you like, you can end this battle immediately with a Master Ball, but if you want to catch it with something more interesting, like a Premier Ball, it is a rough fight.

Once that’s done, Greevil will battle you directly with a full team of Shadow Pokémon. Oh, and did I mention Shadow Moves are super effective against every regular Pokémon type? And if all that wasn’t enough, he also has the 3 Legendary Birds! Yep, this guy's team is absolutely stacked! What a brilliant fight!

Of course, if you thought that 6v7 was brutal, Volo will totally rock your world. The first time I fought Volo, his team caught me totally off guard. I knew as soon as I saw the Spiritomb and heard that classic music track that this was going to feel like a fight against Cynthia.

Cynthia is famously one of the hardest battles in all of Pokémon, being the Champion of the Sinnoh region. Now imagine if she had a Giratina that emerged after she was defeated… and then you had to defeat that Giratina twice, once in its Altered Form, and once in its Origin Form, all without being able to revive or heal your team. Yep, this was a truly unfair 6v8 battle, and it was glorious.

This is what I meant earlier about trainers who cheat. They make for some of the coolest moments in the series. Why would the main villain let you walk all over them and still abide by the rules? They would try to defeat you by any means possible, right?

So having Volo with effectively 8 Pokémon, plus a Legendary, made for one of the craziest and coolest battles of all time. I’m not sure any other trainer will live up to the awesomeness of that final battle! We will have to wait and see!

If you're in search of a true Pokemon challenge, then check out these 10 Hardest Pokemon ROM Hacks Of All Time

UK’s ARIA will test Lumai’s light-based AI servers in a data centre

📅 2026-11-03  |  Read Source →

The Oxford spin-out says its optical chips can cut AI power use sharply. The lab will check that.

ARIA’s Scaling Inference Lab will deploy Lumai’s Iris servers, which use beams of light to run AI calculations, as its first optical platform. Lumai claims big energy savings, but these have not been independently tested. The lab will test the servers in a commercial data centre.

The UK’s Advanced Research and Invention Agency (ARIA) will test servers that use light to run AI models, in a lab it funds, Oxford startup Lumai said on Tuesday.

The Scaling Inference Lab, backed by £50M from ARIA and run by the nonprofit CommonAI, will install Lumai’s Iris servers as its first optical computing platform. It will run them across several machines in a commercial data centre, to see how they cope with real AI models, power and cooling.

Lumai, spun out of Oxford University in 2021, encodes data into beams of light. Its chips do the heavy maths behind AI using light rather than switching transistors, while normal electronics handle the rest. Salience Labs, which also builds on Oxford research, raised $125M last month for optical switches.

The company aims its hardware at “prefill”, the stage where a model first reads a user’s prompt. Ordinary chips would still produce the answer.

“Lumai’s technology is the most mature optical compute platform we evaluated,” said David Lewis, ARIA’s chief science and technology officer.

Lumai claims its approach could cut data centre power use by up to 90%. It also says the system is at least 16 times more energy efficient per request than silicon alone, when paired with standard chips. None of these figures has been independently checked, and testing them is the lab’s job.

The lab already hosts UK startup Oriole, which uses light to link AI chips. Nvidia, meanwhile, has spent billions on light-based connections inside data centres. Another UK inference-chip startup, Fractile, is raising at $6.5B.

Gears Of War: E-Day Revives Classic Versus Mode For 20th Anniversary Celebration

📅 2026-11-03  |  Read Source →

Horde Siege also gets a new limited time mission

After a relatively quiet few weeks for Gears of War: E-Day, The Coalition has just dropped its 20th Anniversary celebration update, reviving a classic Versus mode alongside a new limited time mission for Horde Siege.

Guardian is the Versus mode in question, which has featured in almost every Gears title since Gears of War 2. It's no surprise, then, that the classic mode is getting some love from E-Day. For those who've never played Guardian before, each team starts a round with a 'leader', and it only ends once one leader is dead.

It was a decent mode back in the day, but only if your leader isn't a silly-sausage by getting themselves killed immediately. I'm just glad to see it add more variety to E-Day's Versus suite.

In addition to the returning mode, Horde Siege is getting a new "For the Queen" mission. The limited time mission disabled resupplies at caches littered throughout the map, meaning you'll have to rely on what you go in with, and what you can repurpose from grub corpses.

We've also spotted a few additional 'Anniversary' challenges, skins, banners, and poses in the store too.

‘Hopefully…’ – Andoni Iraola hints at potential Ryan Gravenberch return date after injury blow

📅 2026-11-03  |  Read Source →

Andoni Iraola has provided an update on the injury which has ruled Ryan Gravenberch out of Liverpool’s Champions League fixture against Fenerbahce.

The Merseyside club confirmed on Tuesday morning that the midfielder hasn’t travelled with the rest of the squad for the game in Istanbul tomorrow due to a heel injury and will instead undergo recovery at the AXA Training Centre.

While that obviously comes as a body blow for the Reds for the match against Dirk Kuyt’s side, it appears that the 24-year-old is unlikely to be sidelined for long.

Iraola was prompted for an update on Gravenberch during his pre-match press conference in Istanbul this afternoon, and the Liverpool head coach is hopeful that the Dutchman will be cleared to resume playing quickly.

The Reds boss said (via Liverpool Echo): “It’s a day-to-day thing. It’s a heel injury. He’s had some discomfort. He was warming up against Arsenal and was not 100%. Hopefully he can be available for the weekend, but we cannot say for sure.”

Want more Empire of the Kop coverage? Add us as a preferred source on Google to your favourites list for news you can trust

If Iraola has left the door open for Gravenberch to potentially return against Crystal Palace next Sunday, it suggests that the midfielder’s injury is relatively minor.

The 24-year-old mightn’t make it back in time for the visit to Selhurst Park, but the two-week international window should hopefully enable him to be fully fit for the grudge match against Manchester United later this month.

The Dutchman was criticised for his performance against Chelsea in the Carabao Cup last week and in recent games has been disapponting by his standards, but he remains a vital player to Liverpool and one of the best midfielders in the Premier League when operating at his top level.

Even if he’s cleared to play on Sunday, Gravenberch seems unlikely to start given the form of Alexis Mac Allister and Trey Nyoni in the middle of the park, and he mightn’t be able to feature for a full 90 minutes if he’s touch-and-go for the weekend.

Fingers crossed that our number 38 will at least be available to Iraola when the Reds take on Palace with one eye on revenge for their last-gasp defeat in this fixture last season.

MSRs trade at richest levels in decades as lenders, funds pile in

📅 2026-11-03  |  Read Source →

Mortgage servicing rights are trading at their richest levels in decades, with pristine MSR multiples nearing 7x as more lenders choose to retain servicing and build recapture platforms.  AI Summary

Chicago-based Rate has recently changed its servicing strategy, retaining mortgage servicing rights (MSRs) instead of selling.

“We’ve been retaining all of our MSRs for the last 12 months, so we’re over $50 billion in MSR retention,” CEO Victor Ciardelli said in an exclusive interview with HousingWire. “We really believe in retaining the MSRs, staying close to the customer.”

Rate’s approach reflects a broader trend of mortgage lenders holding onto their portfolios or seeking to add MSRs as a way to recapture borrowers when rates eventually fall.

John Sullivan, managing director of MSR advisory services at Milliman, has seen that shift firsthand. Speaking during IMN’s Mortgage Servicing Rights Conference in New York on Monday, he said he’s having more conversations with clients about retaining their portfolios “unless liquidity is an issue,” and about monetizing them if they are not hedging.

Higher demand for MSRs is not limited to independent mortgage banks (IMBs). Depository institutions are also showing more interest, executives told HousingWire during the conference.

“Banks historically are not great at recapture, but we think that given some changes on the Basel capital front, we’re going to see more bank participation in this space on a go-forward basis, and I think banks will think about just customer acquisition in general, cross-selling in general,” said Larry Goldstone, president of capital markets and lending at BSI Financial Services.

Strategic investors are also willing to grow their portfolios. Rob Williams, co-founder and CEO at Grander Investment Management, a specialized asset manager focused on MSRs, said he’s tactical but sees MSRs as a “spectacular asset” that can reach double-digit returns.

“I can probably be more patient,” he said during the conference, when comparing his approach to the other buyers. “I can be more tactical.”

The buyer base views MSRs through different lenses.

Rich McRedmond, managing director at CrossCountry Mortgage, said that while banks see MSRs as part of their consumer banking strategy, real estate investment trusts (REITs) see them as a pure investment. And mortgage companies see them as a borrower recapture tool “from when they get the mortgage to when they get the next mortgage.”

Conventional, pristine MSRs are seeing 7x multiples amid high demand, a level not reached in more than two decades.

“We’ve seen some pretty extraordinary multiples of late,” Goldstone said. “This is usually an inexpensive asset, and therefore an asset that people want to be able to own. I think you could hedge MSR pretty effectively and still get low-double digits, high-single digit returns up until about the last year. That’s pretty attractive for an asset that really doesn’t have a whole lot of credit risk associated with it.”

Simon Aldrich, senior executive vice president at Freedom Mortgage, believes that at this point, “it’s very difficult to be in this market if you don’t have recapture.”

“In our model, we don’t hedge, so I’m not worried about rates going up or rates going down. Rates go up, I get the pickup at slower speeds. Rates go down, our call center kicks into gear.”

Bayview Asset Management, which acquires MSRs from the bulk and flow markets and uses five different subservicers, has also built out a full recapture strategy, including through its acquisition of Guild Mortgage. Managing director Michael Lau said Bayview has partnered with investors, including sovereign wealth funds and pension funds, that have long-duration strategies.

“MSRs are great because they’re not correlated to the equity or the bond market, and gives them something alternative that also, because we hedge out all the interest rate risk for them, delivers a very consistent return on their money, net of our fees,” Lau said.

Some companies have taken advantage of current pricing to sell MSRs for different reasons, including to pay back debt or raise funds.

In the third quarter, Rocket Companies sold $23 billion of unpaid principal balance (UPB) for $379 million in cash proceeds, retaining the subservicing and recapture services on nearly 70% of the MSRs sold. Analysts told HousingWire the company usually sells production in the wholesale channel due to lower recapture possibility.

Pennymac Financial Services entered into an agreement to sell $29 billion in UPB of low-coupon conventional MSRs, with $490 million of proceeds expected to pay down debt. The transaction is expected to close in the fourth quarter.

Meanwhile United Wholesale Mortgage (UWM) sold about $60 billion — at a roughly 6x multiple — to JPMorgan Chase, sources told HousingWire. The companies declined to comment. This is part of a plan to bring up to $80 billion of MSRs to market in the fourth quarter, with the expectation of being paid more than $1.8 billion in an attempt to reduce leverage.

Newrez President Baron Silverstein said his company is taking a disciplined approach. Across the Newrez portfolio, 60% of loans were originated before or during the COVID-19 pandemic.

“In today’s market, given where we are from a rate perspective, there’s pressure in a competitive environment where MSRs get created — where that gain on sale is from an all-in gain-on-sale perspective on loans that we originate or buy. And when we think about that, we get very selective and stay very disciplined about how we put that capital to work,” Silverstein said.

“When you think about hedging in a market like this, that also requires a lot of capital, and it’s very challenging in these markets today, regardless of the interest rate environment. This has been the case for the last year to year and a half.”

Looking ahead, servicing executives believe that multiples can stay higher as elevated mortgage rates reduce prepayment risks, which supports MSR cash flows. Some of them said multiples could move lower if there’s a liquidity issue due to large bulk sales hitting the market.

Regulation could also play a role. “If Basel III goes through and the banks decide to get back into the space, I think the high-six multiples could be relatively inexpensive,” Goldstone said. “You could see multiples in the 7x-8x category.”

Turn Wendy's Chili Into Game Day Nacho Dip With This Ex-Employee's Simple Hack

📅 2026-11-03  |  Read Source →

Wendy's menu includes many items you might expect to find at a fast food joint: burgers (although it uniquely offers square patties), fries, chicken nuggets, and salads. But the chain is also a standout in the drive-thru world, as it's one of the few major brands to offer customers items like baked potatoes and bowls of chili, the latter of which are truly comforting after a long day when you want something hearty and filling. Wendy's chili can also be useful on game day as the base for a single-serve nacho dip, and all you have to do is add nacho cheese and sour cream.

The creator of this hack — a former Wendy's employee — simply adds the two extra ingredients and gives it a thorough stir, so it's an incredibly easy upgrade to pull off. Both the nacho cheese and the sour cream will mix easily into the chili, provided it's still hot, or at least warm, from the restaurant, with the cheese adding richness and the sour cream lending a welcome tang. Together, they also create a creamier consistency that makes it perfect for dipping.

If it has cooled, you might want to give it a quick nuke in the microwave (in a different vessel). If it's not quite nacho-y enough for your tastes, you can absolutely sprinkle in some smoky taco seasoning. Or dress it up with some diced jalapeños for heat, and top it with green onions to cut through all the richness.

If you head to your local Wendy's to pick up the ingredients for this nacho cheese dip hack, you might run into some confusion when ordering the cheese sauce, as some customers have reportedly had trouble purchasing it because employees couldn't figure out how to ring it up. Fortunately, several Redditors shared that the sauce can be found under Sides, then Cheese Menu, where it's listed as Cheese Sauce. With that information, you should be able to secure the goods (for a small fee), and the cashier will likely think you're a Wendy's whisperer.

Now, while you're ordering, it might occur to you to get something to dunk into your makeshift nacho dip. Fries are the obvious choice, and Wendy's spuds are particularly well-suited to the job, since they're slightly thicker-cut than some of their fast food counterparts, so they can stand up to the chunky chili better. In a pinch, the saltine crackers that come on the side make for a tasty way to scoop up the dip, but you'd be remiss if you didn't also consider the acclaimed spicy nuggets, or Wendy's Tendys, the chain's juicy breaded chicken strips.

The Xbox Nano has a hidden cloud secret

📅 2026-11-03  |  Read Source →

Microsoft’s miniature Xbox controller can connect directly to Xbox Cloud Gaming over Wi-Fi.

Microsoft’s miniature Xbox controller can connect directly to Xbox Cloud Gaming over Wi-Fi.

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Microsoft is about to launch its new Xbox Nano controller tomorrow, and the tiny device has a little secret. The Nano can connect directly to Xbox Cloud Gaming over Wi-Fi to improve latency. It’s a feature Microsoft hasn’t officially announced yet, but one I’ve been anticipating since the Xbox Nano leaked earlier this year.

The Nano will be able to connect directly to Microsoft’s cloud servers to reduce latency, much like how Google’s Stadia controllers used to work. This new cloud connect feature for the Nano is being privately tested on the new Xbox Cloud Gaming website, and it will require you to pair the controller over Wi-Fi to Microsoft’s Xbox Cloud Gaming servers using the Xbox mobile app.

I’m expecting Microsoft to announce this as a preview for the new Xbox Nano controller soon, before it’s fully supported across Xbox Cloud Gaming on the web and on smart TVs. I first started reporting that Microsoft was planning a new controller with Wi-Fi connectivity to reduce Xbox Cloud Gaming latency earlier this year. I’d expect the upcoming Xbox Elite 3 controller will also have the same functionality.

Microsoft had also planned to introduce a direct-to-cloud feature on a new Xbox controller, codenamed Sebile, as part of an Xbox Series X refresh console in 2024. The refresh console was canceled, and it’s not clear when we’ll eventually see a new Xbox wireless controller with direct cloud functionality.

The Range Rover Evoque and Velar Won’t Live Past 2027

📅 2026-11-03  |  Read Source →

But don’t worry if you’re not in the upper 1%; JLR has plans to keep affordable Land Rover models in the mix.

Update, 11/3 1:30pm: Jaguar Land Rover has confirmed to Road & Track that the Velar and Evoque will be discontinued: "Both Range Rover Velar and Range Rover Evoque will continue to be sold in selected global markets where demand remains strong, throughout 2027. Production of both models will end in mid-2027 in line with their natural product lifecycles, as JLR's manufacturing facilities transition to build its next generation of vehicles. Solihull will continue to focus on producing Range Rover, Range Rover Sport and the forthcoming Jaguar Type 01, while Halewood will transition to produce the new Range Rover GT and a new model from the Defender family."

The Range Rover Velar and Evoque could soon be facing the axe, as a new report from Autocar contends that both of Range Rover’s smaller, entry-level models won’t see a new generation.

The report cites Martin Limpert, Range Rover’s global managing director, as saying both the Evoque and Velar will be produced through 2027, but not beyond. The current generation Evoque was revealed all the way back in 2018 and went through a mid-cycle refresh for the 2024 model year. Meanwhile, the Velar is even older, having been revealed in 2017. It too was treated to a mid-cycle refresh for 2024.

The elimination of both the Evoque and Velar would significantly increase the cost barrier to entry for a car with the Range Rover badge on it. It would also leave the “Range Rover” brand with only a few models—those being the traditional Range Rover, Range Rover Sport, and incoming Range Rover GT.

But that might not be such a terrible thing. Instead of targeting the lower end of the market with Range Rover-branded models, JLR appears ready to use both its Defender and Discovery brands for those endeavors. Reports of a smaller Defender model suggest it could easily take the place of the Evoque, and JLR has confirmed a new model of some sort will be joining the Defender line. Meanwhile, the Discovery already comes in around the same price as a Velar.

Both the Evoque and Velar launched as relatively controversial projects, but ultimately led to JLR increasing sales numbers. Applying the Range Rover badge to smaller and more affordable cars was a way to make the brand more accessible, and plenty of folks were happy to jump on the train. It even opened some strange doors, with the Range Rover Evoque Convertible being the weirdest of them all. When the Evoque and Velar finally exit, they’ll join the Discovery Sport that’s confirmed to end production this year.

misha kahn turns upholstery into painting and marble into fabric at friedman benda

📅 2026-11-03  |  Read Source →

the designer discusses his return to hands-on experimentation, where unexpected materials and processes reshape familiar domestic objects.

Misha Kahn‘s latest exhibition, All Intensive Purposes, brings a radical collection of furniture and sculpture to Friedman Benda, marking ten years of collaboration with the gallery. A sofa resembles a pile of painted shapes, and a marble console seems to sag beneath the drapes of its own fabric-like surface.

The works reveal Kahn’s fascination with material experimentation and the unpredictable ways an idea can develop through the process of making. At the show’s opening, designboom spoke with the artist and designer about the thinking behind these unusual objects and his return to more tactile methods of working.

Throughout the New York gallery, familiar objects take on unexpected qualities. Striped plywood returns to tree-like forms, copper is worked into the appearance of loosely knitted fabric. There’s even a ‘lazy river’ circulating through the space.

For much of his career, Misha Kahn has moved between physical craft and digital experimentation, producing furniture through everything from improvised molds to virtual reality. His earlier work often carried the exaggerated, gravity-defying appearance of objects conceived on a computer screen. This time, he describes a shift toward something more immediate.

‘I think, the past handful of years, I really was doing so much work conceptually, digitally,‘ the artist tells designboom. ‘I think this show was really sort of a studio move.‘ Working partly from his studio in the woods, he describes a spiritual shift toward something ‘really tactile,’ with collage and material experimentation becoming central to the new pieces.

The new upholstered pieces offer one of the clearest examples of Kahn’s current approach. Their rounded forms appear assembled from enormous painted fragments, with irregular cushions that overlap and separate. Kahn explains that the process began with a painted composition, which was subsequently translated into wool by textile studio JG SWITZER. The material was then brought back into the furniture-making process, with patterns used to reconstruct the original painted arrangement across the upholstered surfaces.

‘I’ve never gotten that control over the surface,‘ the artist says, explaining that conventional upholstery typically requires working within the limitations of existing fabrics. The new process allowed him to develop the form and its graphic treatment together.

The construction underneath is equally unconventional. ‘We make the frames in the studio, and they’re welded so that the shapes can really flow,‘ Kahn explains. ‘I find that the most satisfying part is sort of the air between the cushions, because we’re so used to seeing a continuous form.‘ Some of the cushions appear loosely attached, raising questions about which parts can actually be moved, while additional pillow elements extend the composition even further.

Perhaps the exhibition’s most unexpected addition is a miniature lazy river, a winding installation that circulates through the gallery alongside a large video projection. The idea came to Kahn with surprising simplicity. ‘I thought, I don’t know why, I think like the beach,‘ he recalls, laughing. ‘I was like, this show needs a lazy river.‘

The video presents an abstract sequence of actions in which materials are repeatedly manipulated and transformed, eventually producing a kind of juice. ‘I wanted to make this video, like, a really abstract version of what it feels like to make objects,‘ he explains, describing the experience of doing something, taking it apart, and putting it back together over and over again.

Kahn designed a series of plastic bottles to accompany the installation, using the familiar manufacturing process associated with commercial water bottles to produce unusual, doubled forms. The arrangement circles continuously, offering a physical interpretation of the exhibition’s title, All Intensive Purposes, which shifts the familiar expression toward the intensity of making, even when the destination remains uncertain.

Kahn has spent much of his career working somewhere between furniture and sculpture. His pieces maintain a recognizable connection to domestic objects, while their unusual proportions and complex surfaces complicate their intended use. In Throws of Passion, for instance, a substantial marble console is carved to resemble draped fabric, its folds appearing almost weightless despite the solidity of the stone.

Asked whether his practice has recently moved further toward sculpture or functional furniture, Kahn describes both as part of the same imagined environment. ‘I feel like I always just wanted to feel like it’s coming from one world,‘ he says. ‘Objects really communicate an idea because I think you understand them as, like, oh, this is what’s in the house, and this is what goes there.‘

He also acknowledges the freedom that comes with making objects beyond the expectations of furniture. ‘I have really enjoyed making sculpture because it just doesn’t have some of the same kind of requirements,‘ he explains. Still, the distinction appears of limited interest to him. ‘I always feel like it should have both. All of my favorite historical figures did both.‘

Cat-sized foxes in Texas are in trouble. Big guard dogs could help.

📅 2026-11-03  |  Read Source →

Published Nov 3, 2026 5:10 PM EST

Two guard dogs could be protecting more than just the livestock grazing on the Rita Blanca National Grasslands in the Texas Panhandle. The working canines may be helping increase the odds of survival for the Lone Star State’s smallest fox.

The swift fox (Vulpes velox) is about the size of a house cat. While not federally listed as endangered, conservationists believe there are fewer than 250 left in Texas. They only occupy about 40 percent of their historic range, from Texas north to Alberta, Canada.

“The swift fox relies on the shortgrass prairie ecosystem once found across the Panhandle and into western Texas,” Haley Sloan, a doctoral student at Texas A&M, said in a profile. “Coyotes can take advantage of the cover provided by the taller vegetation that has grown into these areas, resulting in coyote predation being a leading cause of mortality in swift fox populations.”

Sloan is currently studying if livestock guardian dogs paired with a cattle herd can reduce swift fox mortality. The thought is that the dogs will act as a larger predator, and change the way the coyotes behave and use their habitat.

“The hope is that the dogs’ presence will deter coyotes, thereby reducing swift fox mortality and allowing the foxes to start using areas where the coyotes are being excluded,” Sloan said.

The dogs work in northwestern Texas’ Dallam County, the only known county in the state that still has a swift fox population. The swift fox’s small size and diet of insects, plants, and small rodents also means that they don’t pose any threats to cattle, sheep, and goats.

If incorporating these livestock guardian dogs with cattle herds alters the coyotes’ behavior, Sloan said that it offers two potential benefits. It could improve the chances of swift fox survival, while reducing the threat of livestock predation, especially during the vulnerable calving season.

To monitor the animals’ behavior, Sloan and the other researchers put GPS collars on coyotes and swift foxes ahead of this three-year-long project. The livestock guardian dogs were introduced with the cattle herd this summer. While it is too early to report any initial findings, the team has high hopes.

“I’m happy to report right now the dogs are doing great,” Sloan said. “The producer involved in the project hasn’t lost any calves to predation, and I have hope we’ll have similar positive outcomes for swift foxes in the vicinity of the herd.”

3 Dark Personality Traits Linked To Infidelity

📅 2026-11-03  |  Read Source →

Certain personality traits may help explain why some people betray a partner’s trust.

Certain personality traits may help explain why some people betray a partner’s trust.

Psychopathy, manipulativeness and narcissism are all associated with cheating.

The strongest average link is with psychopathy, closely followed by Machiavellianism.

Narcissism, which involves an inflated sense of self-importance and a need for admiration, has the weakest link.

Impulsivity, emotional coldness and a willingness to manipulate others may help explain why these characteristics are associated with betrayal.

The conclusions come from a review of 11 studies examining dark personality traits and infidelity.

Participants included university students, married couples and adults from the general population.

All three traits were positively associated with infidelity, although the strength of the association varied between studies.

Psychopathic traits include impulsivity, low empathy and a lack of guilt.

Someone with these characteristics may be more drawn to immediate sexual rewards and less concerned about hurting a partner.

A willingness to take risks could also make the possibility of being caught less of a deterrent — impulsivity and deception may help explain this link.

Psychopathy had the largest average correlation with cheating, although its association was close to that of Machiavellianism.

Machiavellianism involves a cynical view of others and a willingness to manipulate people for personal advantage.

In romantic relationships, these characteristics could make deception easier to justify when it serves someone’s interests.

However, a calculating approach may also encourage caution.

Someone concerned with protecting their reputation might weigh the potential rewards of an affair against the consequences of discovery.

This calculated approach could help explain Machiavellianism’s slightly weaker link with cheating.

Narcissism involves feelings of superiority, entitlement and a strong desire for admiration.

Attention from another potential partner may appeal to someone who places great importance on being desirable.

Nevertheless, narcissism had the smallest average association with infidelity and produced a less consistent pattern across the reviewed studies.

An inflated ego alone therefore offers relatively little information about whether someone has been unfaithful.

The broader picture may depend on how personality combines with relationship satisfaction, commitment and access to alternative partners.

Dark traits provide only part of the explanation for why people betray a partner’s trust.

The study was published in the journal Psihologija (Ataman & Yalçınkaya Alkar, 2025).

Psychologist, Jeremy Dean, PhD is the founder and author of PsyBlog. He holds a doctorate in psychology from University College London and two other advanced degrees in psychology. He has been writing about scientific research on PsyBlog since 2004. View all posts by Dr Jeremy Dean

Our Last Chance to Save This Country Begins Today. Let’s Not Waste It.

📅 2026-11-03  |  Read Source →

Don’t even start thinking about the 2028 presidential election. Start thinking about what we’re going to do tomorrow and the day after that.

(Optional Video Accompaniment to This Post)

It is a decade now since the president came down the golden escalator and the country went straight to hell. All we’ve done as citizens of a self-governing democratic republic is squabble over the seating in the handbasket. Until Tuesday. Tuesday was nut-cutting time. Tuesday felt—probably rightly—like the beginning of an extended last chance that the country will have to spend every waking hour pursuing.

Just on Monday, the president closed out his demented road show with a weird address in front of one of his administration’s most conspicuous blunders: the Reflecting Pool. Meanwhile, the Supreme Court, with his carefully manufactured conservative majority, gave the boot to his latest attempt to ratfck the California elections, and the court did so without even a hint of an explanation. Then the president capped a rich, full day by bombing an Iranian island in the Persian Gulf, although I guess he went a little bananas on his little boutique website.

Mama said there’d be days like this. But she never said there’d be so many. And all the rest of them are on us. If I can offer one piece of advice it would be this: Ignore anything written that references the 2028 presidential election. If things break the way the polls say they will, there will be so much concentration required to do what we can to ameliorate the damage this administration has done and is capable of doing over the next two years. Starting the 2028 horse-race coverage this week would be yet another crime against poor, battered journalism. As the late Senator Edward Kennedy told a Democratic convention long ago:

Run through the tape, America. Finish the race.

This Unexpected Antique Is Making a Major Comeback—and It Might Be Hiding in Your Grandma’s Art Collection

📅 2026-11-03  |  Read Source →

This centuries-old style brings fresh character to the walls.

Vintage art adds soul to a space. There’s just something about that story and patina that lends itself to more personality for your walls. While oil paintings and tiny art have certainly had their time to shine, there’s another antique art form that’s taking center stage: They’re called reverse glass paintings.

Popular in India in the 18th and 19th centuries, reverse glass paintings are illustrations that have been hand-painted onto the back of glass, giving them a painterly, colorful look. Annalise Holasz, founder of vintage art gallery Hue + Holasz, says that these works have a “really naïve” quality since each is painted by hand, and the “little variations make each one special.”

Holasz also notes that their frames, often made from reclaimed wood, are particularly special, since they provide a charming, aged, and even battered appearance. These artworks can showcase visuals like flowers, plants, Indian princes, boats, and shells, as Holasz says, “but animals are probably the most common,” and include birds, fish, sea creatures, and jungle animals.

To learn more about reverse glass paintings, we tapped the experts, who are sharing what to look for when starting your own collection.

Alexander Widener, owner of Widener Company, believes that reverse glass paintings are rising in popularity because “they capture the spirit of craft and yesteryear that so many of us are craving in our increasingly digital world.” It also doesn’t hurt that earlier this year, designer Tory Burch gave Vogue a tour of her Paris apartment, where reverse glass paintings were spotlighted in her powder room.

“I think people are drawn to how fun and playful they are,” Holasz says. “There’s often a subject that feels personal, and the combination of vibrant color and a characterful wooden frame appeals.”

Melanie Lissack of Melanie Lissack Interiors believes that reverse glass paintings are trending because they are eclectic, unique, and vintage in style while still having a modern twist.

While current-day pieces can look remarkably similar to the bygone versions, Lissack thinks they just don’t carry the same charm as the antique pieces—as striking as they are. Those in the know don’t look at the artwork first; they look at the frame. For instance, Lissack is on the lookout for slightly worn frames.

Widener points out that if you’re questing after particularly old reverse glass paintings, you should look for bubbles in the glass, a sign that the glass was handblown. This was a standard practice up until the early 20th century.

Also, it can be as simple as buying the motifs you love. For Widener, it’s country scenes and images of animals. He’s also not put off if they don’t have a frame at all.

“If you aren’t heading to Jaipur anytime soon, then Etsy and eBay are great places to look,” Holasz shares. “Sellers restock weekly. I’d browse quite a few listings before choosing, because the frame can make such a difference. They can sell pretty quicky as they are always in demand though, so it’s worth doing some research to shortlist styles you like so you can move quickly when the right thing comes along.”

If you’re searching for the truly antique varieties (made in the 18th and 19th centuries), Holasz adds that these are likely more available at auctions.

Also, don’t count out flea markets and antique shops for these finds. For reverse glass paintings, according to current eBay listings, you can expect to pay as low as $5 all the way up to $5,000. The prices for these paintings tend to be a steal these days since they’re just starting to have a moment, so snatch them up before the prices rise.

While you can certainly give the spotlight to a single reverse glass painting, many designers believe these diminutive pieces look best when hung together in small groups.

As for rooms where these works look their best, Holasz thinks they look “really adorable” in children’s rooms, from nurseries to bedrooms to playrooms. They also act as hardy artwork that can be displayed in kitchens and baths, since, as Lissack says, the paintings are “protected by being on the rear side of the glass.”

Also, don’t be afraid to go a bit rogue with these pieces. For example, Holasz likes to mix them into a gallery wall alongside oil paintings, drawings, and sketches, since the different surfaces create visual interest. And you don’t have to hang them on the wall—they also look great leaned on bookshelves.

Widener, who often scores unframed versions, prefers to display them on wire plate hangers.

“When placed in a window, the light comes through in a really beautiful way,” he says.
